金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 10 月 27 日
上週重點訊息
◼ AI 投資回報疑慮升溫，資金轉向債市避險：市場對大型科技企業 AI 基礎建設投資規模、融資
需求及未來獲利回收能力的疑慮升溫，導致科技股賣壓擴大，10 月 22 日 Nasdaq 指數下跌
約 2.6%。隨著市場風險偏好下降，避險資金轉向美國公債，10 年期公債殖利率單日下降約 19
個基點，創 2023 年 3 月以來最大單日跌幅，並於 23 日回升至 4.99%。市場關注焦點逐漸由
AI 投資規模轉向資本支出效益，後續大型科技企業財報及現金流表現，將成為評估 AI 產業評

價合理性的重要依據。
◼ 中東衝突推升通膨風險：中東衝突持續升溫，伊朗支持的葉門胡塞武裝攻擊沙烏地阿拉伯，引
發市場對原油供應中斷的擔憂，推升布蘭特原油價格於 10 月 23 日升至每桶 116.72 美元，單
週上漲約 6.2%，創 2022 年以來最高週收盤水準。能源價格攀升加劇全球通膨壓力，並壓抑
市場風險偏好。
◼ 法國政治動盪加劇財政疑慮，歐洲主權債風險重新受到關注：法國因生活成本上升爆發大規模
抗議，進一步加深市場對政府財政整頓能力的疑慮。10 月 19 日法國 10 年期公債殖利率上升
約 10 個基點至 4.88%附近，與德國公債利差擴大至約 144 個基點，創 2012 年以來最高收盤
水準，法股單日下跌約 1.5%，歐元同步走弱。市場擔憂政治不確定性可能削弱財政改革進度，
並透過主權債評價影響銀行業資產負債表。後續須關注法國財政政策、歐洲主權債利差及銀行
融資成本變化。
市場展望
◼ 科技股短線宜審慎，中期聚焦基本面穩健的 AI 企業：近期 AI 資本支出、融資需求及評價調整

使科技股波動加劇，但先進製程與半導體設備需求仍具韌性，AI 中長期成長趨勢未改變。隨市
場焦點由成長題材轉向投資效益，企業營收與訂單能見度、自由現金流及資本效率的重要性提
升，具技術優勢、財務穩健及獲利能見度的企業相對有利，而高度依賴外部融資者宜審慎看待。
◼ 美債避險功能重現，中天期債券風險報酬相對均衡：市場風險偏好降溫時，美國公債仍具一定
避險效果。儘管 Fed 貨幣政策路徑與能源價格增添利率不確定性，短天期債券易受政策利率
牽動，超長天期債券面臨財政赤字、國債供給及期限溢價等壓力，相較之下，中天期美債的風
險報酬較為均衡。信用債方面，投資等級企業基本面及現金流仍具韌性，後續仍可持續關注。

美 
國 
企業活動維持擴張，消費信心與房市疲弱，通膨及關稅風險仍存 
經濟 
⚫ 10 月美國綜合 PMI 產出指數初值由 58.4 降至 57.6，企業活動仍強勁擴
張，內需及出口帶動訂單與就業成長；惟油價、關稅及供應鏈瓶頸推升
成本，售價漲幅創 2022 年 8 月以來新高，通膨壓力持續升溫，後續須
關注聯準會政策動向。 
⚫ 9 月美國住宅市場續顯疲弱，建築許可及新屋開工分別年減 3.0%及
7.4%，反映住宅投資動能放緩。儘管多戶住宅許可與完工數回升，但整
體營建活動仍偏弱，後續仍須關注房貸利率、住房負擔能力及營建成本
對市場復甦的影響。 
⚫ 密西根大學公布之 10 月美國消費者信心指數降至 48.0，月減 0.2%、年
減 10.4%，顯示民眾對目前經濟環境仍缺乏信心，雖然對未來景氣的看
法略有改善，但高物價與借貸成本仍讓消費者感到壓力。此外，未來一
年及長期通膨預期分別升至 4.7%及 3.5%，反映民眾仍擔心物價持續上
漲。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 10 月 23 日川普宣布調整加拿大商品 50%關稅清單，10 月 30 日生效，
豁免部分紙製品、機械及包裝材料，並新增地毯、油漆等課稅項目。外
媒認為此舉與期中選舉有關，有助降低部分企業成本，但美加貿易摩擦
仍未化解。 
短評 
⚫ 10 月美國綜合產出指數雖略為下滑，但仍維持高檔，顯示內需與就業持
續支撐企業活動擴張。然而，消費者信心仍處低檔，反映高物價與借貸
成本持續壓抑購買意願，儘管民眾對未來景氣的看法略有改善，但新屋
開工及建築許可同步下滑，顯示消費與住宅投資前景仍面臨挑戰。另一
方面，美國調整部分加拿大商品關稅，雖有助減輕部分企業進口成本，
但雙邊貿易摩擦尚未化解，政策不確定性仍存。

⚫ 市場預期歐洲央行在 10 月 28 日的政策會議將採取觀望立場。

服務價格，通膨黏著性可能進一步提高，為日本央行貨幣政策正常化提

製造業動能與企業信心，後續仍須關注高成本環境對經濟成長的制約。

國際經濟金融週報 
兆豐國際商業銀行 徵信處 
115.10.27 
一、 美國—企業活動維持擴張，消費信心與房市疲弱，通膨及關稅風險仍存 
標普全球(S&P Global)公布，2026 年 10 月美國綜合 PMI 產出指數(Composite 
PMI Output Index)初值由上月的 58.4 降至 57.6，其中服務業採購經理人指數
(Services PMI)由 58.8 降至 57.9，製造業採購經理人指數(Manufacturing PMI)則由
55.9 微升至 56.0，創 2022 年 5 月以來新高，企業活動雖略有放緩，但仍維持強
勁擴張。需求方面，內需穩健及出口改善帶動整體新訂單增速創逾 4 年半新高，
商品出口訂單亦為 2025 年 6 月以來首次增加；企業持續擴增人力，整體就業增
速為 2022 年 6 月以來次高。另一方面，未完成訂單增加、供應商交貨延遲加劇，
加上油價上漲及關稅推升能源、運輸與原物料成本，使企業投入成本維持高檔，
帶動售價漲幅升至 2022 年 8 月以來最高。儘管企業對未來一年營運前景的樂觀
程度略降，但仍處於今年相對高檔。整體而言，美國經濟在內外需及就業支撐下
維持擴張，惟供應鏈瓶頸與成本上升加劇通膨壓力，後續仍須關注物價走勢及聯
準會貨幣政策動向。 
密西根大學公布，2026 年 10 月美國消費者信心指數終值降至 48.0，略低於
上月的 48.1，月減 0.2%、年減 10.4%，並較今年 2 月下降約 15%，顯示消費者信
心持續處於低檔。分項來看，當前經濟狀況指數由 50.9 降至 46.7，月減 8.3%、
年減 20.3%；消費者預期指數則由 46.3 回升至 48.8，月增 5.4%、年減 3.0%，反
映民眾對未來景氣的看法略有改善，但整體信心仍偏弱。調查指出，高利率及購
買負擔壓力使耐久財購買條件下降 18%，拖累當前經濟狀況指數；未來一年企業
景氣預期雖改善約 14%，但仍低於去年同期 16%。通膨預期方面，未來一年通膨
預期由 4.6%升至 4.7%，長期通膨預期亦由 3.4%升至 3.5%，雙雙連續第二個月攀
升，創今年 5 月以來最高水準。密西根大學指出，2025 年關稅政策衝擊及 2026
年伊朗衝突引發的能源價格上漲，使民眾通膨預期再度升溫，且長期預期已明顯
高於 2019 年及 2024 年水準。整體而言，10 月美國消費者信心大致持平，但高物
價與借貸成本仍壓抑購買意願，加上短期及長期通膨預期持續上升，可能增加物
價回穩的難度，後續仍須關注能源價格、家庭消費及通膨預期變化對聯準會貨幣
政策的影響。

用相關內容。

5

2026 年 9 月美國住宅市場建築許可年化總數降至 140.0 萬戶，月減 0.2%、年
減 3.0%，其中單戶住宅建築許可降至 84.5 萬戶，月減 4.0%、年減 4.8%，惟 5 戶
以上多戶住宅建築許可增至 50.3 萬戶，月增 5.9%、年增 1.0%，顯示不同住宅類
型開發規劃表現分歧。新屋開工年化數降至 122.1 萬戶，月減 5.2%、年減 7.4%，
其中單戶住宅開工 85.9 萬戶，較去年同期仍增 2.6%，但多戶住宅開工降至 34.9
萬戶，年減 25.9%，拖累整體營建動能。施工方面，在建住宅維持 128.0 萬戶，與
上月持平但較去年減少 3.3%；完工年化數則回升至 119.2 萬戶，月增 2.2%，主要
受多戶住宅完工月增 14.9%帶動，惟整體仍較去年同期減少 14.0%。整體而言，
美國住宅市場呈現建築許可及新屋開工下滑、在建規模持平及完工略有回升的態
勢，反映住宅營建活動仍偏弱，後續仍須關注房貸利率走勢、住房負擔能力及營
建成本變化對住宅市場復甦的影響。 
美國總統川普 10 月 23 日依據《1930 年關稅法》第 338 條簽署公告，調整部
分加拿大商品加徵 50%關稅的適用範圍，並自 10 月 30 日起生效。此次調整取消
膠合板、部分紙製品、機械與冷凍設備、包裝用木箱及紙箱、新鮮與乾燥海藻等
商品的額外關稅，同時新增閥門、地毯、油漆、洗髮精、可可粉、木製棺材、空
調設備及獨木舟等課稅項目，呈現局部鬆綁與新增課稅並行的政策方向。白宮表
示，此舉旨在減輕加拿大貿易措施對美國企業造成的負擔，並維護國內產業利益。
由於緬因州農業及製造業高度依賴加拿大進口物資，共和黨參議員柯林斯(Susan 
Collins)積極向白宮爭取關稅豁免，外媒亦將此次調整與 11 月 3 日期中選舉連結。
整體而言，新措施有助降低部分美國企業的進口成本，但美加貿易摩擦尚未完全
化解，後續仍須關注雙邊談判及關稅政策對供應鏈的影響。 
二、歐元區—法國動盪短期內難降溫，料將使央行保持審慎觀望態度 
 歐元區 10 月綜合採購經理人指數(PMI)下降 1.3 點至 51.9，抵銷上月的強勁
增幅，但為連續五個月高於 50 的榮枯線，主要係受到法國大規模抗議活動的影
響，使法國綜合 PMI 下降 2.7 點至 48.4；此外，德國綜合 PMI 下降 1 點至 52.8，
亦反映近期通膨壓力增加，使民眾傾向保守消費。若以產業區分，歐元區服務業、
製造業 PMI 分別降 1.3、0.5 點至 51.7、52.9；惟德國製造業 PMI 上升至相當強勁
的 54.2，反映德國政府擴張軍備投資以及基礎建設之需求，持續推動製造業生產
動能。

### Page 7

本報告係根據研究資料及各項可靠數據作成，兆豐銀行保留報告內容所有權利，未經本行許可，請勿引
用相關內容。

6

法國抗爭活動規模逐漸擴大，各大工會與抗議團體已宣布在 10 月 31 日再度
發起大規模遊行，並預告於 11 月 5 日舉行全國性大罷工，抗議主要訴求包括改
善公共服務品質及凍結物價上漲。然而，在財政壓力巨大且國會缺乏穩定執政多
數的情況下，法國政府既無法強力通過重大的財政改革，也難以快速推出安撫反
對派的妥協法案或者公共支出。故部分較為悲觀的機構認為此波動盪可能會延續
至 2027 年 4 月的法國總統大選。受法國局勢影響，近期歐元區各國殖利率普遍
呈現上升趨勢，並對景氣產生暫時性的抑制效果。在此背景下，市場預期歐洲央
行在 10 月 28 日的政策會議將採取觀望立場，後續關注歐洲央行是否將透過政策
訊號或流動性工具，為平息法國主權債務利率而採取行動。 
三、日本—貿易逆差及通膨壓力延續，內需仍具韌性 
 9 月出口雖維持強勁，受半導體製造設備及汽車出口增加所帶動，出口額年增
16.7%；惟進口增幅更為顯著，9 月進口額年增 18.9%，其中原油及液化天然氣進
口額分別大增 51.1%及 39.2%，主要反映中東局勢推升能源價格，以及日本尋求
替代能源供應來源的影響。由於進口額高於出口額，9 月貿易逆差達 5,365 億日
圓，為連續第 5 個月呈現逆差；累計 2026 上半財年（4 至 9 月）貿易逆差亦較上
年同期擴大 98.8%至 2.82 兆日圓。整體而言，出口並未明顯轉弱，近期貿易逆差
擴大主要反映能源進口成本攀升，使進口增幅持續高於出口。 
 9 月全國消費者物價指數(CPI)年增 2.1%，即使政府透過能源及教育補貼等措
施抑制物價，通膨仍高於 2%，顯示實際通膨動能更為強勁。從物價結構來看，石
油、原物料及工資成本上升已逐步傳導至終端價格，漲價範圍由家具、醫療用品
及休閒用品等商品，進一步擴散至房屋維護、公共運輸、住宿及外食等服務項目，
顯示企業成本轉嫁範圍逐步擴大，通膨壓力亦由商品向服務領域擴散。隨通膨持
續高於 2%且漲價範圍擴大，亦為日本央行進一步推動貨幣政策正常化提供支撐。 
 10 月綜合 PMI 持平於 52.3，顯示經濟仍維持穩定擴張，其中製造業 PMI 下
降 0.8 點至 53.3，產出指數亦降至 3 月以來最低，主要反映新訂單及出口訂單成
長放緩，加上稀土金屬等原料供應受限，對生產形成一定壓力，惟生產與訂單仍
處擴張區間，尚未顯示製造業景氣明顯轉弱；服務業 PMI 回升 0.7 點至 52.0，新
業務及出口訂單同步改善，顯示服務需求仍具韌性，為支撐景氣的重要動能。另
一方面，能源、薪資及原物料成本居高不下，使投入價格壓力持續偏高，並逐漸

用相關內容。

影響企業對未來景氣的信心。 
四、中國—第三季經濟成長率回升，但前景仍較為不利 
2026 年第三季 GDP 年增率初估值為 4.5%，較第二季的 4.3%上揚 0.2 個百分
點；累計 1-9 月 GDP 年增率則為 4.6%。第三季的二、三級產業年增率分別較第
二季上揚 0.4、0.1 個百分點至 3.4%，其中製造業、批發零售業年增率分別達 5.9%、
2.3%為主要動能，抵銷建築業年減 4.4%等拖累因素影響。整體而言，第三季 GDP
年增率上揚有部分受到中秋國慶連假落在 9 月末段(去年同期則於 10 月 1 日開
始)，致基期效果發揮部分影響，相關消費動能仍可能會在 10 月出現暫時性的回
落。 
在需求指標方面，(1)1-9 月累計零售額年增率為 1.1%，持平於 1-8 月的累計
年增率。其中 9 月零售額年增率增加 0.7 個百分點至 1.1%，部分受到新手機上市
帶動買氣，9 月通訊器材消費年增率高達 31.1%，加上中秋國慶長假帶動食品消
費年增率增加 1 個百分點至 2.1%所致；(2)1-9 月固定資產投資年減幅維持於 7.2%，
反映供應鏈移轉、政府主導去化過剩產能等因素導致民間投資需求急遽萎縮；萎
縮產業包含化工、醫藥、專用設備、汽車製造業、金屬製品業等；(3)1-9 月房地
產開發投資額年減幅由 19.9%擴大至 20.6%、施工面積年減幅為 12.8%，新建商
品房銷售面積年減幅擴大為 13.2%，房市投資、買氣皆持續低迷。 
受到出口維持高度成長帶動，9 月工業生產年增率較 8 月增加 0.3 個百分點
至 5.5%(累計 1-9 月工業生產年增率持平 1-8 月水準為 5.3%)。依產業別來看，9
月主要受到煤礦開採、化工、化纖等產業擴張影響，主因中秋與國慶長假前發電
業充實庫存水位，促使煤炭企業加快復產，加上油價上漲帶動相關中下游供應鏈
增產；惟高科技產品包含 IC、機器人、新能源車等年增率皆有放慢，反映去年中
秋國慶連假皆落在 10 月，使得去年 9 月工作日較多，致基期相對墊高的影響。 
展望未來，儘管政府 10 月初宣布釋放 5,500 億元人民幣的地方政府債務結存
限額，但額度下達到資金撥付往往需要數月時間，預期對於第四季的經濟影響有
限，使今年全年經濟成長率預估將落在 4.5%附近。此外，受歐盟右派勢力崛起及
保護主義影響，歐盟對中國貿易政策可能持續趨嚴，後續仍須關注雙方談判及溝
通結果，未來若外需動能減弱，預期中國政府將持續透過發展特定高科技產業及
扶植內需來支持經濟成長動能，以減輕房市不振及貿易環境惡化的衝擊。

五、印度及東南亞—新、馬出口保持強勁增速，菲律賓央行大舉升息 
(1)印度: 10 月 HSBC 綜合採購經理人指數（PMI）初值上升 0.8 點至 56.7，其中，
製造業 PMI 略下滑 0.2 點至 54.9、服務業 PMI 則上揚 1.0 點至 56.2，皆保持穩健
擴張態勢。由於美國、英國、中東及孟加拉等地的訂單增加，帶動新訂單及出口
訂單加速成長，製造商採購庫存增速創八個月來最快、成品庫存增速亦創歷史新
高，然而聘僱指數降至今年以來最低、未完成訂單下降，反映現階段人力充足。
另一方面，投入成本漲幅連續第七個月下降並降至近一年低點，產品售價漲幅亦
明顯放緩，通膨壓力緩解，有助提升企業獲利能力及消費者購買力，並且給予印
度央行（RBI）貨幣政策空間。 
(2)新加坡：9 月非石油國內出口（NODX）年增 68.4%，增速史上最快，連續第
23 個月正成長，其中，人工智慧需求強勁，帶動電子產品出口躍增 107.6%；非電
子產品出口亦大幅成長 54.2%，主要由船舶和遊艇組件所帶動。按出口目的地劃
分，對香港出口激增 121.1%、對台灣、韓國出口增幅達 96%，對美出口成長 73.2%，
但對印度出口下降 5.6%。展望未來，新加坡金融管理局在 10 月的宏觀經濟評估
報告中表示，直至 2027 年人工智慧 (AI) 驅動的成長將保持相對穩定，支持未
來幾季經濟成長表現。由於基數較高以及人工智慧相關投資預計放緩，全球利率
上升可能會對新加坡主要貿易夥伴的消費和非人工智慧投資造成壓力，可能會影
響外部需求，薪資成長放緩、招聘謹慎以及運輸成本上升可能會在 2026 年下半
年對可自由支配支出和零售活動構成壓力，預計 2027 年 GDP 成長將放緩。 
(3)印尼：印尼央行（BI）10 月會議連續第四次會議維持基準利率(7 天期逆回購利
率,BI-Rate)於 5.75%不變，貸款便利利率（LFR）維持在 6.50%不變，但將隔夜存
款便利利率（DFR）調高 25 個基點至 5%，旨在提高印尼盾資產的回報率，藉此
吸引並留住外資，同時宣布分兩階段調降存款準備率，11 月 1 日起下調 1 個百分
點至 8%，12 月 1 日起進一步降至 7%，以提供銀行體系流動性。整體而言，印尼
央行爲捍衛印尼盾，在 5 至 6 月間共升息 100 個基點，同時調降外資避險成本，
並採取「資金停留愈久、優惠愈高」的機制吸引外資流入。惟近期國際指數編纂
公司 MSCI 擬將印尼股市由”新興市場”降級為”獨立市場”，引發資金外流疑慮，
央行選擇利率按兵不動，搭配降準提供銀行體系流動性，以兼顧印尼盾穩定與國
內經濟需求，未來 MSCI 檢討進展(最快 2027 年 4 月公布)及印尼盾走勢，將持續
牽動央行後續政策。

(4)菲律賓：菲律賓央行（BSP）10 月 22 日宣布大幅調升基準利率(逆回購利率)2
碼至 5.50%，為 2023 年以來最大幅度升息。央行指出， 9 月通膨率已升至 7.2%，
遠高於 2%-4%的目標區間；同時核心通膨持續攀升，顯示價格上漲已由食品與能
源擴散至整體經濟。BSP 亦擔憂中東情勢推升國際油價、運輸費率調漲，以及聖
嬰現象衝擊農業生產，進一步推高物價。此外，披索貶至歷史低點附近，增加進
口通膨風險，因此採取較激進的緊縮措施以穩定市場預期，貨幣政策重點已由支
持經濟成長轉向優先遏制通膨風險。 
(5)馬來西亞：9 月出口額年增 54.5%至 MYR2,151 億元，其中電子&電氣產品出
口大增 69.2%，持續反映 AI 帶動的強勁需求，礦產品出口亦大幅成長 56.3%，抵
銷農產品出口續跌 5.7%的影響，對台灣、美國出口大增 175.2%、89.9%、對新加
坡出口增幅亦達 50.4%。9 月進口額成長 52.7%至 MYR1,814 億元，受惠出口引
申需求，中間產品、資本貨物進口分別成長 69.6%、20.9%，消費品進口轉為正成
長 18.2%，自中國和新加坡進口分別成長 67.6%、90.7%。進出口相抵，9 月貿易
順差達 MYR337 億元、年增 64.9%。 
六、台灣—外銷訂單創新高，產業景氣冷熱不均 
9 月外銷訂單達 1,142.1 億美元，再創單月新高，年增 62.4%，主要受惠於人
工智慧、高效能運算與雲端服務等應用蓬勃發展，以及消費性電子新品陸續推出，
帶動相關供應鏈維持高檔；加上國際原物料價格上漲，推升整體接單表現。惟各
產業接單動能仍呈分化，科技產品需求強勁，傳統產業景氣則相對疲弱。經濟部
指出，雖有年底節慶補貨潮挹注，但傳統產業部分產能過剩，終端需求仍未見明
顯回升。

## Document 114: 2330台積電 群益(1)

Source PDF: all report pdf/2330台積電 群益(1).pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-11
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

產業速報  
                                        2026 年 11 月 11 日

1 群益投顧 
台積電(2330 TT)投資 OpenAI 及 Anthropic 延續過去參與 Arm(ARM US) IPO 的少數
策 略 股 權 模 式 ， 惟 布 局 由 半 導 體 供 給 生 態 系 進 一 步 延 伸 至 AI 模 型 與 算 力 需 求 端 。 透
過投資 OpenAI 及 Anthropic 各最高 5 億 美 元 ， 增 加 對 未 來 算 力 需 求 及 運 算 架 構 演 進
的 掌 握 ， 作 為 先 進 製 程 與 封 裝 產 能 規 劃 的 參 考 ， 但 不 代 表 取 得 訂 單 或 控 制 權 。 現在台
積 電 的 產 能 和 良 率 優 於 同 業 ， 可是未來美 國 有 可 能 因 環 境 能 耗 與 安 全 疑 慮 對 AI 數據
中 心 及 尖 端 模 型 祭 出 強 硬 監 管 ， 並 取 消 稅 收 補 貼 ， 引 發 市 場 擔 憂 科 技 巨 頭 放 緩 AI 資
本 支 出 ， 間 接 影 響 台 積 電 的 業 績 成 長 動 能 ， 因 此 建 議 台 積 電 Trading Buy。

研 究 員 ： 陳 俐 妍   
ly.c@capital.com.tw

台 積 電 並 非 首 次 策 略 性 投 資 產 業 鏈 公 司 ， 惟 此 次 首 度 將 布 局 延 伸 至 AI 算力
需 求 端 ：  
台積電於 11/10/2026 召 開 董 事 會 ， 分 別 核 准 兩 項 投 資 案 ： 一 是 以 不 超 過 美
金 5 億 元 設 立 投 資 公 司，持 續 進 行 半 導 體 產 業 及 相 關 生 態 系 之 策 略 性 投 資；
二 是 分 別 以 不 超 過 美 金 5 億 元 認 購 Anthropic, PBC 普 通 股 及 OpenAI Group 
PBC 特 別 股，兩 項 直 接 股 權 投 資 合 計 最 高 10 億 美 元。其 中，Anthropic 每股
認購價格將依該公司未來首次公開發行(IPO) 價格而定。值得注意的是，新
設 投 資 公 司 與 直 接 投 資 兩 家 AI 模 型 業 者 為 兩 項 獨 立 的 董 事 會 決 議 ， 目 前 並
無公開資訊顯示兩者具有直接的資金或架構關聯，整體反映台積電一方面
持 續 布 局 半 導 體 產 業 生 態 系 ， 另 一 方 面 首 度 將 直 接 股 權 投 資 延 伸 至 AI 模型
與 算 力 需 求 形 成 端 。  
從歷史脈絡觀察，台積電早在 1990 年 代 即 透 過 InveStar 及 VentureTech 
Alliance(VTA) 等 創 投 架 構 投 資 全 球 無 晶 圓 廠 (Fabless)及 新 興 科 技 公 司，以及
入股 IMS Nanofabrication 約 10%以 布 局 關 鍵 多 重 電 子 束 光 罩 技 術 。 其 中 ，
2023 年 以 不 超 過 1 億 美 元 參 與 Arm IPO 是 此 次 投資最 具 參 考 價 值 的 案 例 ，
台 積 電 當 時 並 非 為 取 得 經 營 控 制 權 ， 而 是 以 少 數 策 略 股 權 維 持 與 關 鍵 IP 生
態 系 的 長 期 連 結 。 此 次 對 OpenAI 及 Anthropic 各 最 高 5 億 美 元 的 投 資 ， 本
質上延續了此種非控制性少數策略股權模式，共同反映台積電並非以大額
資 本 主 導 標 的 ， 而 是 透 過 有 限 資 本 強 化 與 關 鍵 生 態 系 夥 伴 的 策 略 連 結 。  
最 大 戰 略 意 義 ： 策 略 股 權 由 半 導 體 供 給 端 進 一 步 延 伸 至 AI 算 力 需 求 端 ：  
過 去 台 積 電 的 策 略 投 資 主 要 集 中 於 晶 片 設 計 、 半 導 體 IP 及 關 鍵 設 備 技 術 ，
核 心 目 的 在 於 擴 大 半 導 體 供 給 生 態 系，協 助 客 戶 持 續 開 發 並 製 造 先 進 晶 片，
投資 Arm 即 屬 於 間 接 支 持 相 關 架 構 設 計 與 製 造 需 求 的 模 式 。  
相 較 之 下 ， OpenAI 及 Anthropic 位於 AI 產 業 鏈 的 需 求 形 成 端 ， 模 型 能 力 提
升 、 訓 練 規 模 擴 大 及 推 論 需 求 增 加 ， 最 終 將 帶 動 GPU、 ASIC、 HBM 及 資 料
中 心 基 礎 設 施 的 算 力 需 求。因 此，投 資 2 家 模 型 業 者 的 策 略 意 義 並 非 直 接 取
得 晶 圓 訂 單 ， 而 是 增 加 台 積 電 接 觸 及 理 解 未 來 AI 算 力 需 求 變 化 的 機 會 ， 包
括訓練(Training) 與推論(Inference) 需 求 結 構、運 算 架 構 演 進 及 資 料 中 心 基 礎
設 施 需 求 等 ， 作 為 長 期 先 進 製 程 及 先 進 封 裝 產 能 規 劃 的 參 考 。  
相 較 台 積 電 2026 年 預 估 600 億至 640 億 美 元 的 資 本 支 出 規 模 ， 新 設 投 資 公
司的 5 億 美 元 資 金 上 限 僅 約 0.8%；而 直 接 投 資 兩 家 模 型 業 者 合 計 最 高 10 億
美 元 ， 相 較 2 者 各 自 超 過 1,000 億 美 元 的 資 金 規 模 及 高 估 值 亦 屬 有 限 。  
若與 OpenAI 及 Anthropic 既 有 主 要 策 略 投 資 者 相 比 ， 台 積 電 各 最 高 5 億美

### Page 2

產業速報  
                                        2026 年 11 月 11 日

2 群益投顧 
元 的 投 資 規 模 明 顯 較 小 。 過 去 Microsoft(MSFT US)、SoftBank(9984 JP)等業
者對 OpenAI 的 投 資 金 額 達 數 十 億 甚 至 百 億 美 元 級 別 ； Amazon(AMZN US)
及 Google(GOOL US) 對 Anthropic 的 累 計 投 資 亦 達 數 十 億 美 元 。 相 較 之 下 ，
台 積 電 此 次 各 5 億 美 元 的 投 資，顯 然 並 非 以 資 金 主 導，取 得 控 制 權，董 事 會
席次、主 導 公 司 經 營 為 或 追 求 短 期 財 務 回 報 為 目 的，而 是 以 相 對 有 限 的 資 本
建 立 策 略 關 係。惟 實 際 持 股 比 例 仍 須 視 認 購 價 格、股 權 條 件 及 後 續 稀 釋 情 況
而定，目前亦無資訊顯示台積電因此取得參與模型公司技術決策或運算架
構 (Compute Roadmap) 制 定 的 權 利 。  
對 台 積 電 而 言 ， 投 資 AI 模 型 業 者 亦 有 助 於 擴 大 觀 察 未 來 算 力 需 求 的 來 源 ，
而 非 完 全 依 賴 單 一 晶 片 供 應 商 的 產 品 規 劃。無 論 未 來 模 型 業 者 採 用 NVIDIA 
GPU、 AMD GPU、 Google TPU 、 AWS Trainium 或其他自研 ASIC， 只 要 相
關晶片及先進封裝採用台積電技術，即可能轉化為台積電的製程與封裝需
求 ， 策 略 價 值 在 於 增 加 對 需 求 端 運 算 架 構 發 展 方 向 的 理 解 。  
仍 須 面 對 客 戶 中 立 性 、 訂 單 轉 換 及 股 權 評 價 等 風 險 ：  
這 項 投 資 案，仍 有 幾 項 風 險。首 先，台 積 電 必 須 維 持 客 戶 中 立 性。OpenAI 及
Anthropic 分 別 與 Microsoft、Google 及 Amazon 等 大 型 雲 端 業 者 存 在 密 切 合
作 關 係 ， 並 涉 及 NVIDIA、 AMD、 Broadcom 及其他 AI 晶 片 供 應 鏈 。 台 積 電
直接投資特定模型業者，可能使部分客戶關注其資訊隔離及資源配置的中
立 性。此 次 採 取 各 最 高 5 億 美 元 的 少 數 股 權 投 資，且 同 時 投 資 兩 家 主 要 模 型
業 者，有 助 降 低 市 場 對 單 一 選 邊 的 疑 慮，但 台 積 電 後 續 仍 須 維 持 嚴 格 的 商 業
資 訊 保 密 及 客 戶 中 立 原 則 。  
其 次 ， 股 權 投 資 不 等 於 取 得 訂 單 或 產 能 承 諾 。 OpenAI 及 Anthropic 並非直
接 採 購 晶 圓 的 客 戶，其 算 力 需 求 仍 須 透 過 CSP、GPU 及 ASIC 供 應 商 轉 化 為
台 積 電 的 晶 圓 與 先 進 封 裝 需 求。因 此，這 筆 投 資 本 身 並 不 代 表 產 能 長 約、保
證取貨(Take-or-Pay) 承 諾 或 未 來 訂 單 保 證 ， 實 際 效 益 仍 取 決 於 AI 算 力 需 求
的 持 續 成 長 ， 以 及 相 關 晶 片 供 應 鏈 採 用 台 積 電 技 術 的 程 度 。  
最後，AI 模 型 業 者 目 前 仍 處 於 高 資 本 投 入 及 快 速 競 爭 階 段 。 若 未 來 模 型 服
務 競 爭 加 劇、商 業 化 速 度 低 於 預 期，或 雲 端 業 者 的 AI 資 本 支 出 回 報 率 下 降，
導 致 算 力 投 資 放 緩 ， 均 可 能 影 響 OpenAI 及 Anthropic 的 估 值 及 後 續 融 資 能
力 ， 進 而 帶 來 台 積 電 股 權 投 資 的 評 價 波 動 風 險 。  
整 體 而 言，台 積 電 此 次 策 略 並 非 改 變 晶 圓 代 工 本 業，而 是 延 續 過 去 以 少 數 策
略 股 權 連 結 關 鍵 產 業 生 態 系 的 做 法 。 若 說 投 資 Arm 主 要 是 鞏 固 晶 片 如 何 被
設 計 與 製 造 的 供 給 生 態 系 ， 投 資 OpenAI 與 Anthropic 則 是 進 一 步 接 觸 為 何
需 要 更 多 晶 片 與 算 力 的 需 求 形 成 端。對 台 積 電 而 言，以 相 對 有 限 的 資 本 投 入
增加對 AI 產 業 長 期 需 求 變 化 的 理 解 ， 其 策 略 意 義 可 能 高 於 單 純 的 財 務 投 資
報酬。 
11/2026 台 積 電 董 事 會 決 議 如 下 ：

產 能 ；2.建 置 及 升 級 先 進 封 裝 、 成 熟 及 /或 特 殊 製 程 產 能 ；3.廠 房 興 建 及 廠 務

認 購 價 格 將 依 該 公 司 首 次 公 開 發 行 之 價 格 而 定 ； 以 及 於 不 超 過 美 金 5 億元

台 積 電 10/2026 營收 5,868.9 億元，MoM+14.65%， YoY+59.70%：  
台積電公告 10/2026 營收 5,868.90 億 元 ， MoM+14.65%， YoY+59.70%，創
歷 史 新 高 。 先 前 台 積 電 法 說 會 預估 4Q26 營收 523~543 億美元，
QoQ+11.94%~+16.22%，中 間 值 +14.08%。在 1 美元兌 新 台 幣 31.8 元 基 礎 上，
新 台 幣 營 收 為 1.66 兆元~1.73 兆元，QoQ+13.43%~+15.56% 。 以 此 推 估 ，
11/2026 和 12/2026 單 月 平 均 營 收 在 5,381 億元~5,699 億 元 ， MoM-2.89%~-
8.31%。  
高 階 製 程 持 續 帶 動 台 積 電 營 收 成 長 ， 台 積 電 預 估 4Q26 毛 利 率 將 介 於 65%-
67%，以 中 間 值 66%計 算，較 3Q26 下降約 0.4 個 百 分 點。主 要 是 因 為 N2 技
術 的 快 速 爬 坡，預 期 將 稀 釋 毛 利 率 約 3 個 百 分 點，此 稀 釋 將 被 領 先 技 術 的 強
勁需求與持續的成本改善( 含生產力提升) 部分抵消。預估 4Q26 稅後 EPS 
32.64 元。2026 年 EPS 111.39 元。 
儘 管 消 費 性 ( 如 PC、 智 慧 型 手 機 ) 與 價 格 敏 感 終 端 市 場 面 臨 零 組 件 價 格 上 漲
與 總 體 經 濟 不 確 定 性 之 挑 戰 ， 台 積 電 對 2027 年 對 此 抱 持 審 慎 樂 觀 態 度 ， 但
受惠於領先先進製程之技術差異化與廣泛客戶基礎，及代理人 AI(Agentic 
AI) 推動 AI 助 理 普 及 、 開 源 模 型 降 低 使 用 門 檻 ， 進 一 步 刺 激 CPU 與 網 路 晶
片 需 求 ， 預 估 台 積 電 2027 年 稅 後 EPS 147.10 元 。 長 期 而 言 ， 台 積 電 憑 藉 在
先 進 製 程 與 先 進 封 裝 的 高 市 佔 率，實 質 基 本 面 依 然 強 勁。然 而，美 國 選 舉 後，
有 可 能 因 環 境 能 耗 與 安 全 疑 慮 對 AI 數 據 中 心 及 尖 端 模 型 祭 出 強 硬 監 管 ， 並
取 消 稅 收 補 貼 ， 引 發 市 場 擔 憂 科 技 巨 頭 放 緩 AI 資 本 支 出 ， 間 接 影 響 台 積 電
的 業 績 成 長 動 能 ， 因 此 建 議 台 積 電 Trading Buy 。

4 群益投顧 
  【 投 資 評 等 說 明 】  
  評等 定義 
  強力買進(Strong Buy)  首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 ≥ 35%  
  買進(Buy)  15%≤首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 <35%  
  區間操作(Trading Buy) 5%≤首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 <15%

中立(Neutral)  
無 法 由 基 本 面 給 予 投 資 評 等  
預 期 近 期 股 價 將 處 於 盤 整  
建 議 降 低 持 股

【 投 資 評 等 調 整 機 制 】  
  投 資 評 等 係「 首 次 」給 予 特 定 個 股 投 資 評 等 時，其 前 一 日 收 盤 價 相 較 3 個月
目 標 價 之 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 計 算 而 得 。 個 股 投 資 評 等 分 為 四 個 等 級 ， 定 義 如 上 。
爾 後 的 投 資 評 等 係 依 循 「 首 次 評 等 」 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。   
「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」 及 「 區 間 操 作 」 均有 upside 目 標 價 。 差 別 僅 在 於 ，
首 次 評 等 時 潛 在 報 酬 率 不 同 。 「 中 立 」 則 無 目 標 價 。  
一 旦 我 們 給 予 特 定 個 股「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」或「 區 間 操 作 」之 投 資 評 等，
就 是 責 任 的 開 始，爾 後 將 透 過 各 式 研 究 報 告 作 定 期 性、持 續 性 追 蹤 基 本 面 及
股 價 變 化 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。  
停 止 推 薦 情 境 ：  
1. 達 目 標 價 。  
2. 雖 未 達 原 訂 目 標 價 ， 但 檢 視 基 本 面 、 訊 息 面 、 籌 碼 面 等 多 方 訊 息 ， 研
判 股 價 上 漲 空 間 已 然 有 限 ， 將 適 時 出 具 降 評 報 告 。  
3. 推 薦 後 股 價 不 漲 反 跌 ， 亦 將 出 具 降 評 報 告 。

【免責聲明】 
  本 研 究 報 告 僅 提 供 予 特 定 人 之 客 戶 作 為 參 考 資 料「 非 經 同 意 不 得 轉 載 」。我
們 並 不 確 保 此 資 訊 的 完 整 性 與 正 確 性，投 資 人 應 了 解，報 告 中 有 關 未 來 預 測
之 陳 述 可 能 不 會 實 現，因 而 不 應 被 依 賴。而 且 此 報 告 並 非 根 據 特 定 投 資 目 的
或 依 預 定 對 象 之 財 務 狀 況 所 撰 寫 出 來 的，因 此，此 研 究 報 告 的 目 的，既 非 對
投 資 人 於 買 賣 證 券、選 擇 權、期 貨 或 其 他 證 券 相 關 之 衍 生 性 商 品 提 供 詢 價 服
務，亦 非 作 為 進 行 交 易 的 要 約。投 資 人 應 注 意 到 相 關 證 券 之 價 值 及 收 益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

### Page 1

Wistron

Reiterate Buy: 3Q26 beat, better margins; 
AI momentum intact; cut PO to NT$250

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 250.00 TWD | Price: 174.50 TWD

3Q26 beat: solid margins on favorable product/client mix 
Wistron’s 3Q26 GM/OPM of 5.90%/4.24% came in above BofAe of 5.36%/3.66% and 
consensus of 5.55%/3.70%. We attribute the strong margins to greater sales scale 
(+28% QoQ, +102% YoY; 1%/8% above BofAe/consensus), more favorable product/client 
mix in AI business, and better results from Wiwynn, mainly supported by general/ASIC 
server momentum (see also Wiwynn 3Q26 result note). Thus, OP was 17%/24% above 
BofAe/consensus. With non-operating loss of NT$6.2bn (including net interest expense 
and FX loss), NP was 16%/27% above BofAe/consensus (Exhibit 3).  
AI order momentum intact; ongoing project ramp-ups 
We see no change in Wistron’s AI order momentum into 4Q26 and 2027-28. We expect 
4Q26/1Q27 sales to rise 11%/8% QoQ, driven by GPU/CPU rack project ramp-ups, with 
rising ASP and spec upgrade. We also see potential sales upside from more new 
client/project wins in 2H27-2028. Although the GM decline trend is inevitable for AI 
server ODMs, we expect Wistron to post solid core OPM (i.e. excluding Wiwynn) into 
2027-28, with rising operating efficiency and more diversified client/project base. On 10 
Nov, the company announced additional capex plans of subtotal around NT$12.8bn for 
adding facilities and equipment in Taiwan, mainly to support new AI-related businesses, 
and another US$17.8mn for expanding server manufacturing capacity in Mexico.  
Trim PO to NT$250 with a lower P/E set-up; reiterate Buy  
We lift our 2026-28E EPS by 7-21% (but 2027-28E EPS is still 8-9% below consensus) 
to reflect the 3Q26 results beat, solid AI momentum, and sustained core OPM. Yet, we 
lower our PO to NT$250 (12x 4Q26-3Q27E EPS) from NT$265 (15x 2H26-1H27E EPS), 
with the valuation base rollover and a lower P/E multiple. We now apply 12x (1SD above 
historical average since 2022; Exhibit 8), vs. prior 15x (1.5SD above) on valuation de-
rating across AI supply chain amid macro uncertainty. Nevertheless, we reiterate Buy on 
Wistron in view of its solid AI sales momentum with a broadening client/project base.

Company Sector 
IT Hardware

Company Description 
Wistron is one of the top-5 ODMs globally. Server/storage 
and PC business accounted for 73% and 24% of sales in 
2025, respectively. Key server/storage customers include 
Dell, Lenovo, HPE, Cisco, Nvidia, AMD, Intel. Key NB 
customers include Dell, Lenovo, Acer. Wistron was spun off 
from Acer (2353 TT) in 2000 and was incorporated in May 
2001. It went publicly listed on the Taiwan stock exchange 
in Aug 2003. It is headquartered in Taipei, Taiwan.

Investment Rationale 
We assign a Buy rating to Wistron in view of improving 
sales/profit mix, solid outlook from Wiwynn, high exposure 
to server business after exiting from low-margin iPhone 
assembly business, and improving core OPM. We believe 
Wistron will benefit from Dell's strong demand in Nvidia AI 
GPU/CPU rack as the key supplier.

### Page 7

Price objective basis & risk 
Wistron (WICOF) 
Our PO of NT$250 is based on 12x 4Q26-3Q27E EPS. We use 12x valuation multiple 
(around 1SD above historical P/E average since 2022) in view of high server mix, a key 
position in AI server supply chain with exposure both to component/rack business, and 
ongoing core OPM improvement.

Upside risks to our PO are (1) stronger server market demand, especially for AI, (2) 
stronger PC demand, (3) share/order win in AI server business, and (4) favorable FX 
trend.

Downside risks to our PO are (1) softer AI server demand due to AI GPU short supply or 
supply chain hiccup, (2) weaker PC market demand amid inflation/macro uncertainties, 
(3) GM pressure due to higher labor/logistics cost, (4) more intensified competition in 
NB and server business, and (5) unfavorable FX movement (USD/RMB and/or USD/NTD) 
to weigh on GM and/or non-operating items.

FUNDAMENTAL EQUITY OPINION KEY: Opinions include a Volatility Risk Rating, an Investment Rating and an Income Rating. VOLATILITY RISK RATINGS, indicators 
of potential price fluctuation, are: A - Low, B - Medium and C - High. INVESTMENT RATINGS reflect the analyst’s assessment of both a stock’s absolute total return 
potential as well as its attractiveness for investment relative to other stocks within its Coverage Cluster (defined below). Our investment ratings are: 1 - Buy stocks 
are expected to have a total return of at least 10% and are the most attractive stocks in the coverage cluster; 2 - Neutral stocks are expected to remain flat or 
increase in value and are less attractive than Buy rated stocks and 3 - Underperform stocks are the least attractive stocks in a coverage cluster. An investment 
rating of 6 (No Rating) indicates that a stock is no longer trading on the basis of fundamentals. Analysts assign investment ratings considering, among other things, 
the 0-12 month total return expectation for a stock and the firm’s guidelines for ratings dispersions (shown in the table below). The current price objective for a 
stock should be referenced to better understand the total return expectation at any given time. The price objective reflects the analyst’s view of the potential price 
appreciation (depreciation). 
Investment rating Total return expectation (within 12-month period of date of initial 
rating)

Buy ≥ 10% ≤ 70% 
Neutral ≥ 0% ≤ 30% 
Underperform N/A ≥ 20% 
R2Ratings dispersions may vary from time to time where BofA Global Research believes it better reflects the investment prospects of stocks in a Coverage Cluster. 
INCOME RATINGS, indicators of potential cash dividends, are: 7 - same/higher (dividend considered to be secure), 8 - same/lower (dividend not considered to be 
secure) and 9 - pays no cash dividend. Coverage Cluster is comprised of stocks covered by a single analyst or two or more analysts sharing a common industry, 
sector, region or other classification(s). A stock’s coverage cluster is included in the most recent BofA Global Research report referencing the stock.

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가현저한 저평가 - 기업.pdf

### Page 1

00

삼성전자 (005930/KS) 
현저한 저평가 
3Q26 영업이익 106 조원 예상 
삼성전자의 3Q26 영업이익을 106조원 (+18% QoQ)으로 예상한다. 이는 기존 당사 
전망치 111 조원 대비 4.5% 하향된 것이다. 원/달러 환율 가정을 기존 1,450 원에서 
1,400 원으로 3.4% 하향, MX/NW 사업부의 적자 폭을 확대 반영 (-0.5 조원 -> -
1.4 조원)했기 때문이다. 주가의 핵심인 메모리 판가는 DRAM +17%, NAND +15%
로 오히려 예상을 소폭 상회할 것으로 예상된다. 출하는 수요 강세에도 불구하고 재고 
부족, HBM 확대에 따른 제한적 공급 여력으로 DRAM +4%, NAND +7%의 기존 예
상을 유지한다. HBM 은 분기 최대 출하를 경신하기 시작할 것이다. 또한 AI 강세로 파
운드리 수주 및 가동률의 동반 상승이 지속 중이다. 성과 충당금 반영 등에 따라 단기 
흑자전환 가시성은 제한적이나, DS 사업부 전체적으로 강화되고 있는 국면이다.

2027 년 568 조원 (+55% YoY) 전망 유지. 환율 하락 반영 
SK증권은 삼성전자의 2027년 영업이익 전망치를 568조원으로 유지한다. 원/달러 환
율 가정을 기존 1,440 원에서 1,340 원으로 7% 하향했음에도, 실적 전망을 유지하는 
이유는 AI 메모리 수요 강세 속 공급 부족 심화, LTA에 따른 Dual market 효과에 의
한 Commodity 메모리 가격 강세, 2027년 HBM 판가 강세 (+103% YoY) 와 출하 
강세, 수익성 회복의 동반, 파운드리 적자 폭 축소의 본격화 전망 때문이다. 2027년 메
모리 출하는 DRAM +22%, NAND +16%, 판가는 DRAM +38%, NAND +28%
를 예상한다. 시장에서 우려하는 메모리 가격 하락, 감익 시나리오는 없을 것이다.

차별적 실적과 주주환원 본격화. 현저한 저평가 
SK증권은 메모리 업황과 삼성전자의 주가가 현저히 저평가 되어있다고 판단한다. 삼성
전자의 2027E P/E는 4.2X에 불과하며, 이익 창출력은 글로벌 최상위권이다. 주주환
원 정책 또한 기존의 FCF 50% 환원이 향후에도 유지될 것으로 가정한다면, 2027년 
193 조원, 2028 년 222 조원으로 현재 시가총액 (우선주 포함) 대비 각각 11%, 13% 
수준에 육박한다. 성장을 위한 투자를 확대하고 있음에도, 대규모의 환원이 가능하다. 
LTA는 AI 시대 메모리 수요의 질과 실적 가시성을 제고시키며, 이는 주주환원의 지속
가능성을 의미한다. 메모리 가격의 높은 기저, LTA 물량의 계약 가격 적용을 감안하면, 
가격 상승률 자체는 안정화될 것이다. 다만 실적 안정성, 가시성, Duration을 확보하기 
시작했다는 점이 훨씬 중요한 가치이며, 아직 시장의 Playbook에 없을 뿐이다. 시대는 
변하고 있고, 시장도 변해야 한다. 투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 610,000원을 유지한다. 
2026-10-06

반도체. 한동희 / donghee.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-8826

Key: 환율 가정 하향에도 27 년 이익 전망 유지. 공급 부족 심화 
Step: 글로벌 최상위 이익 창출, 주주환원. 현저한 저평가

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
It was already our view that the RBA was likely done hiking
the cash rate after the November increase. Since then, our
conviction on that call has strengthened. Later today we
will publish the November Market Outlook with revised
forecasts. GDP growth, the labour market and housing

market have all been revised down in 2027.
Underlying inflation has been revised down slightly in the
second half of 2027, bringing the year-ended figure for
calendar 2027 more decisively inside the RBA’s 2–3% target
range than our previous forecast round. The unemployment
rate forecast has been revised up to peak above 5%. Based on
this revised forecast profile, an increase in the cash rate from
here would be unlikely and – we would argue – undesirable.

A range of considerations underlie this forecast revision. The
housing market has undershot our forecasts over the past
couple of months. Turnover and financing activity have been
coming in under our earlier expectations. Mortgage loan
inquiries have taken another leg down in October after the
September rate hike, after finding something of a level in prior
months, with the additional impact of the November rate hike
still to come.
We are also seeing early signs of a turn in the consumer.

Consumer income and spending have so far been quite
resilient to pressures from fuel prices and rising interest
rates. But our Westpac Card Tracker is starting to show that
discretionary services spending is turning down again in Q4,
joining the discretionary goods category that was already quite
weak. We would not over-emphasise this point, but it does
imply that the risks of further surprising resilience are fading.
Subsequent to the November Monetary Policy Board (MPB)

meeting, business survey data has shown weaker conditions
and a sharp decline in forward orders. They also show
further evidence that current inflation is mostly a non-
labour cost shock that is not being fully passed through to
consumer prices.
As we noted following the November MPB meeting, oil
and fuel prices are already materially lower than the track
assumed in the RBA’s forecasts. Dated Brent is nearly one-
fourth lower than on 28 October, the date the RBA’s forecasts

were finalised, and spot Brent is around 15% lower. This
significantly lowers the risk of a new wave of pass-through of
fuel costs to other prices, as well as easing consumer inflation
perceptions and expectations.
An RBA peak of greater conviction
Forecast changes
• A full update to our forecasts has been published in our
November Market Outlook.
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
In the media conference following the November meeting,
Governor Bullock flagged that it was a good time to “take

stock” of the policy tightening to date. Both we and the
market read this as a signal that the MPB would pause at the
December meeting. The 6:3 split vote at the meeting also
suggests that some external members are more attentive to
downside risks than the internal members.
A rate hike in February 2027 cannot be ruled out but it is
looking even less likely given signs in the data and surveys.
The MPB will at least discuss a hike in February. The staff and

senior officials remain sensitive to inflation risks and have a
downbeat view of supply capacity growth.
Given what we are seeing in the economy now, though,
a rate hike in 2027 would require either a material new
inflation shock that has not yet occurred, or would
constitute a policy error on the part of the RBA (something
that we will never forecast as a base case, knowing the talents
and capabilities of the staff and the Board). We concur with

the dissenting voters in the MPB that the downside risks need
more attention.
Policy is clearly restrictive, and the risk of overdoing the
inflation correction is growing. And by the time we get to
February, we think there will be more evidence that the
domestic economy is slowing materially, and the inflation
outlook with it.
“A deeper housing downturn and early signs of
cracks in the consumer sector point to an even
softer 2027."

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: cost of living front and centre
the labour market while showing significant concern over
the strength and persistence of inflation, focusing on the
passthrough of energy and technology input inflation. His
conclusion for policy was clear: "if, as I expect, the monthly

rate of inflation improves in October, I am leaning toward
supporting an increase in our policy rate of 25 basis points
at the FOMC's December meeting"; but "if the October CPI
inflation data out later today are similar to the past two
monthly readings—with core CPI inflation around 0.3 percent
or even higher—then a stronger policy move may be needed".
Regional Fed Presidents Goolsbee, Logan and Hammack also
kept the focus on inflation risks and stated or implied the FOMC

needs to act. Market participants have responded to this week’s
updates, pricing a 25bp hike in for the December and January
meetings, and a cumulative 3.5 hikes by September. We believe
this pricing overstates the need for policy tightening given core
inflation ex shelter remains relatively benign at 2.4% on a six-
month annualised basis and the labour market is balanced; but
it will take time for price concerns to subside.
While we agree that the US economy is strong in aggregate,

US households are under pressure from the cost of living, and
their investment decision making is constrained by borrowing
costs. Highlighting this, US existing home sales fell 5.5% in
October after a downwardly revised 1.0% gain in September,
leaving the level of sales below the cycle lows of 2009 and
2010. Our November Market Outlook provide a full update on
our expectations for the US and the world.
In Australia, the Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment Index

fell 2.7% to 78.2 in November, its weakest reading since
late-2022 and a level historically associated with periods of
severe economic stress. Renewed cost-of-living and interest
rate pressures were front and centre following another lift
in inflation, including sharply higher petrol prices, and the
RBA’s November rate increase. Mortgage holders are feeling
the squeeze most acutely, with sentiment across this group
falling to a record low. Consumers have become noticeably

more downbeat on the near-term economic outlook – the
relevant sub-index dropping 10.2% to be 22% below its
long-run average. Job-loss fears also continue to edge up.
Businesses are similarly troubled. The latest NAB business
survey showed confidence slipped further to –15 in October as
conditions held near their weakest level since the pandemic.
Of concern for the outlook, forward orders fell to –12, also
the weakest read since the pandemic, and capital expenditure

intentions deteriorated. Purchase costs continue to grow
materially faster than selling prices, further compressing
already tight margins.
Housing provides a clear example of how caution and tighter
financial conditions can jolt activity. The value of new dwelling
finance approvals plunged 15% in Q3, the largest quarterly
decline in the series’ history, taking approvals 25% lower year-
to-date. Investor lending bore the brunt of the adjustment,
falling 30% over the quarter as higher rates combined

with the housing tax changes announced in May. Owner-
occupier lending also weakened, however, with first-home
buyers proving particularly sensitive to the deterioration in
affordability and market conditions.
Taken together, the data points to a further loss of momentum
across the domestic economy, consistent with Westpac’s
expectation for subdued growth and concern over downside
activity risks.
Meanwhile, in the US, October’s headline CPI met our

expectations but at 0.4% was a touch above the consensus
view. Energy and food both contributed meaningfully to the
result, but core inflation was also marginally stronger than
forecast, +0.3%. Core goods inflation has accelerated over the
past four months to average a gain of 0.2% as businesses look
to pass tariffs, tech and energy input cost inflation through.
Shelter meanwhile continued to rise at an above-average pace,
gaining 0.3% in October.

The market impact of the October CPI report was accentuated
by hawkish remarks from Fed Governor Waller prior to the
release. He expressed confidence in economic activity and
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics
Ryan Wells, Economist
Illiana Jain, Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
Post election, where are we left?
This week markets and the economy needed to digest
the General Election results, as well as data on how the
economy is tracking through the final months of the year. A
centre-right coalition looks more likely to ultimately prevail,

although it may be a while before we know for sure and
longer again before a stable government emerges. Watch
this space as paths to victory for the centre-left remain.
Economic data are consistent with a still-strong external
sector but a weaker domestically-generated growth
impulse. Our upcoming Economic Overview next week will
examine these trends in more depth.
The real star of the show this week was the outcome of the
2026 General Election, which delivered an election night lead

to the centre-right coalition of the National, ACT and NZ First
parties. The preliminary result was a 67-57 seat margin in an
enlarged 131 seat parliament. Hence the centre-right won on
election night with a slender one-seat margin, given that 66
seats are required to form a majority. Financial markets and
the business sector generally cheered this outcome given their
preference for a centre-right government. However, the margins
are tight and there is the potential for the result to switch around

when the final vote count becomes available on November 27.
National leads Labour by narrow margins in two electorates
(Wairarapa and Hamilton East) and could lose those seats
when all is said and done. Also, special votes tend to swing
more to the left than the right historically and hence could
boost the centre-left bloc. The star performer of the election
was the Opportunity Party which debuted in parliament with
7 seats. The centre-left would require their support to get

over the line in all scenarios, whereas the centre-right might or
might not need them depending on how the special votes and
final electorate results play out. There seems a clearer path to
victory for the centre-right – especially if they can come to an
agreement with Opportunity – but clients should be wary of
complacency (which we note may be a risk).
A further complication is that the election left the incumbent
National Party in a much-weakened position as their coalition

partners picked up seats at their expense, and because their
much-reduced share of the party vote (29.2%) meant that
no National Party list MPs made it into parliament. Several
seasoned and senior government ministers needed a list seat
to make it into the new government including Finance Minister
Nicola Willis. Even if the final results confirm a centre-right
coalition win, there will be fierce debate within the coalition
on which party staffs key ministerial roles. ACT party leader

David Seymour is making a strong play for Finance Minister,
which Prime Minister Christopher Luxon is fiercely resisting.
There is the potential for tough and protracted coalition
negotiations to play out well into the Christmas period.
Indeed, I spoke on a post-election panel with an ex-National
Party senior minister this week who expressed the view that
it might not be until Waitangi Day (February 6) before a new
government is formed. Should anything like this play out then

the implications for economic and market uncertainty could
be significant. The initial relief rally in the New Zealand dollar
might prove premature in that scenario.
For interest rate markets, the main conclusion drawn is that as
a centre-left government seems a lower probability prospect,
then so also is the likelihood of a return to a ‘dual mandate’
for the RBNZ. Chief Economist Paul Conway made remarks
this week suggesting ongoing concern with inflation risks,

suggesting he is still in the hike camp at this stage. This has
seen markets firm up short-term OCR expectations such that a
25bp OCR increase in December is now seen as high probability
(85-90% currently). Bond markets cheered the prospect of
potentially tighter fiscal policy that could come with a centre-
right government. The spread between the 10-year government
bond and swap spread narrowed 4 basis points over the week
and the 10-year government bond yield differential to the US

fell to -49 basis points – the most negative since 2019.
Economic data showed further evidence of the two-speed
economy that is showing above-trend growth yet at the same
time feels quite unsatisfying to most voters. The low exchange
rate, generally robust global demand and tight supply
conditions in soft commodity markets have been delivering
consistently strong export returns for a couple of years now.
This week we saw another very strong GDT Pulse auction that

propelled whole milk powder futures higher – up some 20-25%
on year ago levels in USD terms. Significant upside risk for our
2026/27 Farmgate Milk Price forecast of $9.75/kgMS now exists,
which we will be delving into more deeply in our Economic
Overview to be released on Wednesday. The manufacturing
sector appears to remain in a solid state, with the manufacturing
PMI slipping a touch in October to 54.9 from 56.3 in September.
Short-term visitor arrivals have resumed their prior strong

uptrend, growing almost 3% for a second consecutive month
in September and almost 10% higher than a year earlier.
While arrivals from key European markets remain well below
year-earlier levels – likely reflecting the disruption to travel
through key Middle Eastern hubs along with higher jet fuel
prices – this has been more than offset by a very sharp lift in
arrivals from China. Indeed, Chinese arrivals in September
were almost two-thirds higher than the same month last year.

Michael Gordon
Senior Economist

### Page 5

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
5
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
And in the September quarter, arrivals from China surpassed
their pre-pandemic level for the first time. The number of
Australian citizens visiting New Zealand grew just under 6%yr
in September, but growth momentum from New Zealand’s
number one source market (a 30% share) does appear to have

slowed somewhat in recent months.
Less positive trends were evident in the more domestically
orientated areas of the economy. The most concrete indicator
of the NZ economy – concrete poured – dropped back a
touch in the September quarter, while consumer spending
in fell 0.7% in October. We have concerns on the quality
of the consumer spending data given that bank-sourced
data indicates a rise in consumer spending in October. But
nonetheless it’s probably reasonable to infer that higher fuel

costs in October crimped household budgets.
Housing market data for October were also week. House sales
fell and are down almost 22% y/y, while house prices fell in
October for the fifth consecutive month to be down almost
2% y/y. The weak performance of the housing market is likely
another factor weighing on consumer spending and residential
investment. That’s another topic we will be taking a look at in
next week’s Economic Overview.
Net migration flows for September confirm still modest

population growth. Arrivals of non-New Zealand citizens
continues to drift higher while departures declined a touch.
The net outflow of New Zealanders remains relatively high but
is declining, as signs of firmer employment growth emerge in
New Zealand at the same time as the Australian labour market
shows signs of weakening.

### Page 6

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.6
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: Oct Labour Force – Employment Change (000s)
Nov 19, Last: –39.3, Westpac f/c: 30
Market f/c: 27.5, Range: 9.9 to 42
Employment fell by 39.3k in September, well below expectations
for a 20k lift. Some of this weakness may reflect an unwind of
a possible residual seasonality stemming from recent changes

to the survey design, which saw August come in stronger.
Under the monthly noise, the labour market is loosening
gradually as jobs growth runs slower than population growth.
While some leading indicators point to near-term stability
– such as job ads – business surveys and detail within the
employment data point to some downside risk.
For October, we have pencilled in a lift in employment of +30k,
which would see employment growth hold steady on a three-

month average basis.
AUS: Nov Westpac Housing Sentiment (index)
Nov 17, Last: 85.3
The Westpac Housing Sentiment Index that appears in our
regular Housing Pulse reports will now be published on a
monthly basis a week after Westpac-Melbourne Institute
Consumer Sentiment is released. The Index is a composite
based on housing-related sentiment measures from the survey
and has proven to be an extremely useful predictor of turning
points in housing markets, both nationally and across states.

At 85.3, the Oct monthly Index read was holding near historic
lows, well below the long run avg of 100. The Nov update will
capture reactions to the RBA’s latest interest rate rise and the
deepening and broadening housing downturn. The release
will also include results from two new questions added to
the regular survey on consumers’ property buying and selling
intentions over the next twelve months.
AUS: Q3 Wage Price Index (%qtr)
Nov 18, Last: 0.8, Westpac f/c: 1.0

Market f/c: 1.0, Range: 0.9 to 1.1
The Wage Price Index (WPI) rose 0.8% in Q2, leaving annual
growth at 3.2%. Private sector wages continued to decelerate,
in line with a softer labour market, while public sector wages
provided a bit of an offset.
The Fair Work Commission's (FWC) 2026 Annual Wage Review
handed down a larger-than-expected 4.75% increase in award
wages. This will have a noticeable impact on the Q3 WPI, as the
wage increases stemming from the review are typically passed

on during the September quarter.
We have pencilled in a rise of 1.0% for Q3, lifting the annual
pace to 3.4%yr. Detail on wages growth by bargaining stream
will be closely watched for evidence of broader diffusion
beyond arrangements directly linked to the FWC's decision.

### Page 7

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.7
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: Oct Labour Force – Unemployment Rate (%)
Nov 19, Last: 4.7, Westpac f/c: 4.7
Market f/c: 4.7, Range: 4.6 to 4.8
Once again, the surprise in employment coincided with a surprise
in labour force participation, which fell 0.2ppts to 66.8% from
its near cycle high of 67.0% in August. This translated to a 26.2k

decline in the size of the labour force, which was less than the
drop in employment. As a result, the unemployment rate ticked
slightly higher in September, from 4.65% to 4.72% (although it
was steady at 4.7% to one decimal place), a new cycle high.
For October, we have the participation rate steady at 66.8%,
which together with the forecast rise in employment, would
see the unemployment rate also hold steady at 4.7%. Early
signs of rising retrenchments are a key signal to watch over the

coming months, especially if new hiring across other areas of
the economy starts to dry up.
NZ: Oct Selected Consumer Prices
Nov 17
Stats NZ’s monthly Selected Prices provides an update on
around half of the full CPI, including many of the more volatile
items. In terms of the big groups, we’re expecting a 0.1%
rise in overall food prices, with fresh produce prices typically
soft at this time of year. We also expect continued softness
in housing rents. However, the key focus will be on transport

costs. Petrol prices are expected to be up 11%. And we’ll be
watching to see if high global fuel prices are contributing to
further increases in airfares after recent large increases. There
is related uncertainty around holiday accommodation costs
(which drive much of the month-to-month volatility in overall
prices).
NZ: Q4 RBNZ Survey of Expectations (%yr)
Nov 17, Inflation 2yrs Ahead, Last: 2.34
With an easing in headline inflation in the September

quarter, we’re likely to see some easing in near-term inflation
expectations (one year ahead). However, longer term inflation
expectations, particularly the closely-watched two years
ahead measure, are likely to remain broadly steady. While
interest rates are moving higher, businesses are continuing to
report pressures on operating costs. In addition, despite their
recent easing, oil prices remain elevated. The only time longer-
term inflation expectations have moved materially below 2.3%

was in the mid-2010s. That followed an extended period when
inflation was running below 2%.

## Document 317: CCA macro and credit_ Stronger credits, more selective opportunities

Source PDF: all report pdf/CCA macro and credit_ Stronger credits, more selective opportunities.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-17
Pages: 32
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

CCA macro and credit November 2026

1

CCA macro and credit 
Stronger credits, more selective opportunities

Contents  
• Armenia  
(ARMEN) 
• Kazakhstan 
(KAZAKS) 
• Azerbaijan 
(AZERBJ) 
• Kyrgyzstan 
(KYRGYZ) 
• Uzbekistan 
(UZBEK)

The Caucasus and Central Asia (CCA) region has become a more resilient, better 
rated and more accessible market for investors. But, following a strong rally, 
improving fundamentals no longer guarantee attractive returns. In this report, we 
link sovereign ratings, strategy and macro resilience, while initiating coverage of 
Kyrgyzstan. 
Sovereign ratings: Improvement is increasingly priced in 
The CCA has stood out for its improving sovereign ratings despite a series of external 
shocks. Stronger balance sheets, economic reforms and greater political stability have 
supported upgrades and reduced perceived risk. But markets have moved ahead of the 
rating agencies: 5Y USD spreads already imply ratings at least one notch above current 
levels across the region. Further upgrades may therefore have less effect on bond prices, 
particularly in countries where spreads are already tight. 
Kazakhstan and Azerbaijan already have strong investment-grade profiles, leaving less 
room for further spread tightening. Uzbekistan remains our preferred sovereign credit, 
with reforms, fiscal discipline and privatisation creating room for further rating 
improvement and a broader investor base. Armenia looks more vulnerable to 
disappointment: positive rating outlooks sit alongside twin deficits and continued 
reliance on external inflows. Kyrgyzstan’s recent upgrade shows that the positive rating 
momentum is spreading beyond the region’s more established sovereign credits. 
Macro: Resilient, but increasingly divergent 
Our 2027 calls remain constructive on growth and currencies, but sources of resilience 
differ. High core rates are manageable while domestic real rates, sovereign buffers and 
capital inflows hold up, but a potential portfolio flow reversal is a risk. 
• Armenia: Benefits from strong inflows and progress in the peace process, although 
twin deficits and an expensive currency leave less room for disappointment. 
• Azerbaijan: Exceptional sovereign assets and high oil prices protect the peg, while 
weak oil production and limited diversification constrain growth. 
• Kazakhstan: Combines high real rates and better market access with persistent twin 
deficits and greater sensitivity to foreign portfolio flows. 
• Kyrgyzstan: Rapid infrastructure-led growth and an improving sovereign rating are 
offset by overheating, opaque external flows and growing state-led investment. 
• Uzbekistan: Combines high real rates, privatisation inflows and potential recovery of 
gold exports, but overheating and twin deficits prevent a structural appreciation call.

### Page 3

CCA macro and credit November 2026

3 
CCA macro and credit: Stronger 
credits, more selective opportunities 
This report serves two purposes. First, it follows publication of ING’s 2027 Global Outlook, 
which looks at how political, energy, technology and trade tensions could shape the 
global economy and markets over the coming year. Second, it continues our regular 
series of regional macro and credit reports, bringing together economists and sovereign 
strategists to assess the region from both a fundamental and an investor perspective. 
Our previous regional macro and credit report was published in March 2026, as the war 
in the Middle East was entering its early stages. We focused then on the sensitivities, ie, 
how the shock might impact on the region through commodity prices, global risk 
appetite, trade and imported inflation. Now, we are taking stock of how that resilience 
has held up and, more importantly, how the region is positioned for 2027. 
This edition also introduces a new format. As with our Directional Economics CEEMEA 
series, we complement the regular country coverage with a feature article examining a 
common regional theme. For this report, we focus on sovereign ratings. Ratings capture 
several of the changes taking place across the CCA; stronger sovereign balance sheets, 
institutional and structural reforms, better market access and, increasingly, the question 
of how much fundamental improvement is already reflected in asset prices. 
There are other common regional themes we could have explored. Capital market 
development and improving international access are becoming increasingly important, 
particularly in Kazakhstan and Uzbekistan. The interaction between commodity prices, 
currencies and capital flows is changing as foreign participation grows. Infrastructure 
investment and the expanding role of the state and SOEs are shaping growth and fiscal 
risks, while geopolitical fragmentation continues to redirect trade, remittances and 
financial flows. In the interest of focus, we leave these as themes for future editions 
rather than trying to address them all here. 
Lastly, the report marks two further changes to our regular regional coverage. We move 
from defining our research under the CIS (Commonwealth of Independent States), an 
intergovernmental political and economic organisation, to CCA (Caucasus and Central 
Asia) a geographic and economic grouping, bringing our terminology closer to the way 
the region is increasingly referred to internationally. And we add Kyrgyzstan, taking our 
core coverage from four to five economies. Its exceptional growth, infrastructure push, 
rapid financial deepening and improving sovereign-rating story make it a natural 
addition, while overheating and unusually complex external accounts provide plenty of 
questions for investors. 
The pages that follow bring these elements together through the ratings feature, a 
regional macro outlook and five country chapters.

Dmitry Dolgin 
Chief Economist, Russia and CCA 
dmitry.dolgin@ing.de

James Wilson 
EM Sovereign Debt Strategist 
james.wilson@ing.com

### Page 4

CCA macro and credit November 2026

4 
CCA sovereign ratings: The next leg 
of the upgrade cycle? 
The Caucasus and Central Asia region has absorbed a difficult combination of high global 
yields, commodity volatility and geopolitical fragmentation better than its frontier-
market label might suggest. However, resilience across countries has not been uniform. 
Armenia has relied on boosted external inflows, Azerbaijan on sovereign assets and 
energy prices, Kazakhstan on real rates and market access, and Uzbekistan on growth, 
gold and reform. But underlying these factors has been a general trend of improving 
macro fundamentals, reflected in sovereign rating upgrades. 
Our base case remains constructive, in terms of the outlook for further improvement in 
fundamentals, although upgrade momentum is gradually slowing. Set against this is the 
problem of relatively expensive valuations in the hard currency space, with credit 
spreads already tight compared to current rating levels. We would therefore be more 
selective in picking out opportunities, with Uzbekistan our favoured sovereign credit 
given potential for further underlying reform momentum. More broadly, the improved 
macro environment should be supportive for the wider investment case – improving 
credit quality can broaden international market access at both the sovereign, quasi-
sovereign and corporate level, allowing opportunities for increased issuance and offering 
a wider investor base. At the same time, reforms and macro stability should also 
continue to increase investor interest in local currency markets. 
The rating story so far 
In recent years, the Caucasus and Central Asia (CCA) region has been a standout among 
EM sovereigns in terms of improving credit fundamentals, with the countries seeing their 
reward of rating upgrades and reduced risk premium in market pricing. Azerbaijan, 
Armenia, Kazakhstan and Uzbekistan have generally each seen 1-2 notches in upgrades 
to their average credit ratings over the past five years or so. This has come despite the 
array of external shocks for the region, including the Covid lockdowns, Russia-Ukraine 
war, trade/tariff tensions and war in the Middle East. Some of the improvement can be 
ascribed to positive spillovers from the global story, including higher commodity prices, 
trade re-routing and financial inflows. But there have also been institutional and 
structural reforms, along with improved political stability, robust growth and stronger 
sovereign balance sheets. Outside of these four ‘core’ countries in our coverage, recent 
entrant into the EM debt universe, Kyrgyzstan, has also seen a rating upgrade to B2 this 
year by Moody’s, showcasing its potential for further catch-up.

### Page 5

5 
Looking ahead, prospects are still skewed more positive (we have positive outlooks at 
Armenia, Azerbaijan and Uzbekistan), but we expect the momentum to slow. Uzbekistan 
looks like the country with the most potential to continue its upward trend, given the 
picture of structural reforms, while we are more sceptical regarding Armenia’s continued 
improvement, given some elevated external vulnerability and lingering political risks, 
despite it currently having positive outlooks at all three main agencies.  
Country level views  
Fig 2 CCA ratings by Moody’s, S&P and Fitch: An overview

Source: Moody’s, S&P, Fitch, ING

Kazakhstan: The nation’s rating profile has seen some modest improvement in recent 
years, with an upgrade by Moody’s to Baa1 in 2024 and to BBB by S&P this year. This has 
come on the back of resilient economic growth and gradual fiscal consolidation, despite 
signs of sporadic disruption to exports via the CPC pipeline.  
Large external assets and low debt provide a strong floor but the rating is constrained by 
inflation, commodity dependence, governance weaknesses and an uneven macro-policy 
framework. Rating upside would require firmer fiscal discipline, better coordination 
between fiscal and monetary policy, lower and more stable inflation, stronger 
governance and tangible diversification. The most relevant downside risks are sustained 
fiscal expansion, erosion of National Fund assets or disruption to key oil export routes. 
Our view is that the ratings should remain stable at current levels. Valuations in the hard 
currency space are already rich compared to rating levels, in particular with strong 
technical factors supporting front-end spreads. We would therefore expect lower 
investor sensitivity even in the event of further rating upside, although further ratings 
improvement may matter more for Kazakhstan's local market than for already-tight 
hard-currency sovereign spreads. High real yields, growing domestic issuance, stronger 
foreign participation and progress towards international settlement infrastructure are 
gradually broadening the potential investor base. This creates an important funding 
opportunity for the sovereign and quasi-sovereign sector, although greater foreign 
participation also increases FX sensitivity to global portfolio flows. 
Azerbaijan: Recent history has seen Azerbaijan upgraded to full Investment Grade at 
both Moody’s and Fitch amid signs of reduced reliance on hydrocarbons, improved 
institutional quality and progress in peace negotiations with Armenia. 
The sovereign balance sheet already compares strongly with investment grade peers, so 
further upside is less about debt reduction and more about the quality of the policy 
framework. The strongest triggers would be more predictable fiscal management, 
reduced reliance on sovereign wealth fund transfers, deeper monetary transmission, 
greater transparency and visible non-oil diversification. A durable peace process with 
Armenia would provide an additional institutional and geopolitical dividend.

### Page 6

CCA macro and credit November 2026

6 
We expect continued strength for the external and fiscal balance sheet, although see 
less likelihood of further rating upgrades beyond the current BBB- level. Recent years 
have seen decent progress on institutional categories within the World Bank WGI, such 
as government effectiveness, but corruption control and rule of law appear likely to 
remain, holding back further progress. 
We see diversification as the main constraint on further fundamental improvement 
rather than a near-term threat to creditworthiness. Large sovereign assets and 
continued hydrocarbon revenues provide substantial protection, but weak oil 
production, continued reliance on gas (despite broadening geography of gas exports) 
and subdued private investment leave potential growth around 2-3% in the absence of a 
stronger non-oil investment cycle. Further ratings should therefore depend increasingly 
on institutions and private-sector diversification rather than additional balance sheet 
accumulation. 
Uzbekistan: Uzbekistan has been upgraded by all three agencies in recent years and 
maintains one positive outlook, at Fitch. The upgrade cycle has been driven by 
institutional strengthening, rapid growth and fiscal repair, along with beneficial gold 
prices. 
The next leg depends on implementation; completion of energy subsidy reform, 
adherence to deficit and debt ceilings, tighter control of SOE and public-private-
partnership liabilities, credible privatisation and continued development of monetary 
and exchange-rate frameworks. Faster external borrowing or reform slippage would 
challenge the story. 
We can see the potential for the reform story in Uzbekistan to continue, although the 
next leg will depend increasingly on execution rather than headline growth. Continued 
fiscal restraint, SOE reform, privatisation and more disciplined management of 
contingent liabilities could support further rating convergence. Importantly for capital 
markets, these reforms are also generating a broader pipeline of sovereign and 
corporate issuance, potentially making Uzbekistan the region's deepest new-asset-
creation story. 
Armenia: Strong progress since 2022 has seen upgrades from B+ to BB-, with positive 
outlooks across the board. This has come on the back of easing political risks related to 
Azerbaijan, along with strong growth momentum and a sharp improvement in the 
government balance sheet due to increased capital and migrant inflows.   
Positive outlooks put the focus on whether strong growth can become broader and 
more durable. Upgrade evidence would include continued reform delivery, export and 
employment gains, fiscal consolidation that stabilises debt, lower borrowing costs 
through deeper domestic capital markets, and sustained normalisation with Azerbaijan 
and Turkey. Risks centre on renewed geopolitical tension, political disruption or a fading 
of post-2022 growth support without replacement by productivity-led investment. 
We view the picture as somewhat less positive, with persistent twin deficits and close 
economic ties with Russia important vulnerabilities. Russia accounts for around 80% of 
Armenia’s gas imports, one-third of Armenian foreign trade and roughly two-thirds of 
gross remittance inflows. Our base case remains gradual diversification rather than 
abrupt decoupling, but the continued reliance on Russian-linked flows helps explain why 
we are somewhat more cautious on further rating upside than the agencies' positive 
outlooks suggest. 
We expect ratings are more likely to stabilise at their current BB- level, although more 
genuine progress in normalising relations with Azerbaijan could offer some upside. We 
expect growth to moderate towards around 5% over the medium term, with the budget

### Page 7

CCA macro and credit November 2026

7 
deficit broadly around 3% of GDP. External deficits should remain sizeable enough to 
leave Armenia reliant on continued capital and remittance inflows. We view the 
composition of external inflows funding the nation’s current account as something of a 
vulnerability to watch and see current hard currency valuations as overly optimistic.  
Kyrgyzstan: The rating trajectory has turned positive; Moody’s upgraded the sovereign 
from B3 to B2 in October 2026, following a Positive outlook in 2025, citing rapid growth, 
higher income, improving diversification and a declining debt ratio. 
The next upgrade triggers would be a durable strengthening of reserves and the balance 
of payments, narrower structural current account deficits, continued fiscal discipline, 
higher per capita income and broader economic diversification. Downside triggers 
include disruption to Russia-linked trade or remittances, tighter sanctions enforcement, 
persistent off-budget support to SOEs and state banks, rising contingent liabilities, or 
renewed political instability.  
While the potential catch-up story to the rest of the region is of interest to investors, we 
expect further upside requires a more structural shift towards more sustainable growth, 
along with a reduction in structural external vulnerabilities. There also remains a clearer 
potential downside scenario compared to the rest of the region, given signs of economic 
overheating, large external vulnerabilities and increased borrowing needs for SOE capital 
injections. We therefore see the story as more balanced for now, despite the recent 
positive momentum.   
Why the rating story matters for markets 
While our base case is relatively optimistic for the fundamental and ratings trajectory 
for the region, this does not automatically translate into expectations for strong 
performance for hard currency sovereign debt in the region. Generally speaking, 
investors have moved ahead of rating agencies in terms of pricing in fundamental 
improvement. Current market pricing at five-year tenors in the dollar bond space implies 
ratings of at least one notch higher across the board, BB+ in Uzbekistan and Armenia, 
BBB in Azerbaijan and A at Kazakhstan. This suggests that further rating upside should 
have a more limited market impact in terms of spread tightening, in particular for 
Kazakhstan and Azerbaijan that are both already IG rated. 
Fig 3 5Y spreads vs composite rating

Source: Refinitiv, ING

In our 2027 macro outlook for the region (10 November 2026) we highlighted a potential 
upside scenario that could see markets start to price Uzbekistan like an Investment 
Grade credit, seeing demand broaden beyond traditional frontier and high-yield 
investors.

As a wider point, beyond simply expecting tighter credit spreads in the hard currency 
sovereign space, further upward momentum in the ratings space should still be 
supportive for widening the investor base for the CCA region, allowing more international 
issuance in the primary market at the sovereign, quasi-sovereign and corporate level. It 
should also encourage more investment on a broader scale (beyond hot-money and 
speculative portfolio flows), while continuing to increase investor interest in local 
currency markets. Kazakhstan has been placed on Index Watch-Positive for potential 
inclusion in JP Morgan’s GBI-EM local currency debt index and is currently part of the 
new frontier local-currency government bond index (GBI-EM Edge), while Uzbekistan has 
seen its soum-denominated sovereign international bonds included in the GBI-EM.

CCA macro and credit November 2026

9 
CCA macro: Resilient, but 
increasingly divergent 
The region’s improving sovereign ratings reflects genuine progress. But, as the preceding 
ratings discussion indicates, investors have already priced in much of this improvement. 
In our view, the key macro questions are what is underpinning the resilience of individual 
economies, how durable those supports are and where their trajectories are starting to 
diverge. 
Our 2027 macro outlook remains constructive on regional growth and currencies. This 
fits into ING’s global view of another potentially resilient year, despite continued power 
struggles around politics, energy, technology and trade. The CCA region has so far 
navigated this difficult global setting better than might have been expected, benefiting 
from strong domestic demand, commodity revenues, remittances, redirected trade and 
capital inflows. Positive real rates and generally strong sovereign buffers should continue 
to limit the transmission of higher core rates into regional currencies and monetary 
policy. Kazakhstan and Uzbekistan retain room for cautious rate cuts, Azerbaijan should 
remain broadly on hold, while Armenia and Kyrgyzstan face greater tightening risks. 
This resilience is not unconditional. Kazakhstan and Uzbekistan need to preserve 
sufficiently attractive real rates to support demand for local assets, while Armenia 
remains dependent on external inflows and Azerbaijan on hydrocarbon revenues. A 
reversal of portfolio flows would test currencies and protection from imported inflation, 
particularly where external or fiscal deficits remain sizeable. Geopolitics is another 
source of risk; tighter sanctions enforcement, greater scrutiny of regional trade and 
financial flows, or pressure from larger neighbours could reduce the benefits the region 
has drawn from fragmentation. 
The individual country stories increasingly reflect different combinations of these 
strengths and vulnerabilities. Armenia continues to benefit from remittances, tourism 
and capital inflows, but twin deficits, Russia exposure and an expensive dram leave less 
room for disappointment. Azerbaijan has the region’s strongest sovereign balance sheet 
and substantial protection from higher oil prices, although declining oil production and 
limited private sector diversification constrain medium-term growth. Kazakhstan 
combines strong sovereign assets, high real rates and improving international market 
access, but persistent twin deficits and growing reliance on portfolio inflows make the 
tenge more sensitive to global positioning. 
Kyrgyzstan, the newest addition to our coverage, represents the region’s most dynamic 
story. Infrastructure investment, construction, trade and rapid credit expansion have 
produced exceptional growth, stronger revenues and improving headline debt ratios. Yet 
double-digit growth increasingly resembles overheating; inflation is above target, real 
rates are low, monetary transmission is weak and external accounts remain difficult to 
interpret. Large trade deficits, dependence on Russia-linked remittances, opaque 
financial flows and growing state and SOE-related investment obligations mean that 
macro credibility still needs to catch up with headline performance. 
Uzbekistan remains our preferred medium-term growth and reform story. Privatisation, 
high real rates and capital inflows support the soum and the expanding investable 
universe. However, overheating, twin deficits and depleted liquid sovereign-fund buffers 
prevent us from turning near-term currency resilience into a structural appreciation call.  
The country pages examine these increasingly distinct growth, policy and external-
balance stories in greater detail.

Economic overview  
Armenia has remained resilient to the global and regional challenges of 2026, 
supported by renewed inflows of visitors, remittances and capital. Foreign-policy 
developments and solid finances provide additional support to sentiment and the 
balance of payments. However, this resilience increasingly relies on continued 
external inflows, while persistent twin deficits and close economic links with Russia 
remain important vulnerabilities. Strong domestic demand and external cost 
pressures have also pushed inflation above target and prompted the region’s first 
rate hikes this year.  
While we remain constructive on Armenia’s near-term macro outlook and assets, 
growing exposure to foreign political and flow-related risks, alongside an 
increasingly expensive AMD, reduces the margin for error. 
Economic activity supported by the new wave of external inflows  
GDP growth remains elevated at c.7% YoY on a 4Q rolling basis. Some softening in 
investment-heavy industries in 9M26 has been offset by stronger activity in 
consumption, financial services and real estate. Armenia is benefiting from additional 
visitor arrivals and financial inflows, with net remittances rising from a typical c.6% to 
c.8-9% of GDP.  
Around two-thirds of gross remittance inflows originate in Russia, making it a key 
variable for Armenia’s growth outlook. Assuming foreign policy continuity, gradual 
moderation of GDP growth towards c.5% pa, alongside a fiscal deficit, remains our base 
case. A material disruption to Russia-related flows of people, goods (including Russia’s 
parallel imports) and capital remains a downside risk and a factor to watch. The 
medium-term question is whether improving regional connectivity and Western 
investment can increasingly replace the exceptional Russia-linked inflows of recent 
years as a structural growth driver. 
Fig 10 Armenia: GDP growth components by production  Fig 11 Armenia: GDP growth vs remittances

Source: National sources, CEIC, ING  Source: National sources, CEIC, ING

Household activity appears well supported by a combination of growing income, also 
thanks to remittances, fast lending growth and some rebound in state spending. 
Meanwhile, corporate activity is more subdued in 2026 compared to the 2025 spike as it 
remains heavily correlated with foreign trade and is subject to volatile regional politics. 
Russia still accounts for around one-third of Armenia's exports and imports, leaving 
corporate activity exposed to changes in bilateral relations.

Fiscal policy: Still supportive despite some recent consolidation 
The trailing budget deficit has narrowed to around 2.5% of GDP from 3.7% in the 
previous two years. Revenues have outperformed, mainly thanks to strong collection of 
consumption-related and corporate profit taxes, while spending growth has moderated, 
particularly in defence. We expect the deficit to gravitate back towards 3% of GDP over 
the medium term, but the commitment to overall fiscal discipline appears secure. 
Persistent deficits imply a steady issuance pipeline. We continue to expect the 
authorities to prioritise domestic borrowing to limit currency risk and develop local 
markets. Robust economic growth is improving debt sustainability despite rising nominal 
debt, leaving more room for potential external issuance as well. 
Fig 14 Armenia: Key budget parameters  Fig 15 Armenia: Key public debt parameters

Source: National sources, CEIC, ING  Source: National sources, CEIC, ING

Inflation and monetary policy: Growing CPI risks triggered a precautionary hike 
Inflation has hovered around 4% YoY for most of 2026. Relatively low import exposure to 
developed markets and c.5% YoY dram appreciation have helped contain price pressures 
from strong domestic demand and higher global commodity prices. Meanwhile, the 
deteriorating global inflation outlook, combined with regional and domestic risks, has 
raised concerns over further inflationary pressures among households, corporates and 
policymakers.  
With CPI likely to remain above the 3% target for longer, the Central Bank of Armenia 
made two consecutive 25bp hikes in September and November to 7.00%. We expect one 
further 25bp hike by year-end, although we do not see this developing into a prolonged 
hiking cycle.

Balance of payments and exchange rate: FX is supported, but increasingly expensive 
The dram has continued to show above-expected resilience to global capital volatility in 
2026, as it is supported by rising capital inflows, remittances, comfortable reserves and 
favourable investor sentiment linked to the peace process with Azerbaijan and positive 
rating trajectory. This continues to offset Armenia’s sizeable current account deficit, 
helping keep the currency stronger than implied by long-term valuation metrics.  
However, Armenia remains dependent on continued external flows, while close 
economic links to Russia remain vulnerabilities, even if the recent trade data points to a 
reduction in Russia-Armenia trade. Recent EU trade liberalisation covers around 80% of 
Armenian exports to the bloc. The immediate macro impact is likely to be limited, 
however, as the EU currently accounts for only around 12% of Armenia’s goods exports. 
We therefore expect the AMD to stay resilient to a potential global risk off in the near 
term but see limited room for further appreciation and continue to view longer-term 
risks as skewed towards gradual depreciation.

Sovereign credit strategy 
This year has seen a continuation of fundamental improvement for Armenia, with 
positive rating outlooks at all three agencies, and elections that passed without major 
surprises. While there remains the potential for further positive catalysts from the 
ongoing peace process with Azerbaijan, conflict in the Middle East leaves Armenia in a 
more vulnerable position, given the increasing reliance on external inflows.

Economic overview  
Azerbaijan remains the CCA region’s financially strongest sovereign, supported by 
large and growing fiscal and monetary reserve assets, low public debt and a robust 
external balance sheet. Higher hydrocarbon prices have strengthened the near-
term fiscal and external outlook and supported confidence in the non-oil economy. 
However, the strength of the sovereign balance sheet contrasts with a much less 
impressive underlying growth story; declining oil production, limited diversification 
and subdued private investment continue to cap potential growth at around 2-3%. 
Inflation remains manageable for now, but imported price pressures warrant caution. 
We therefore remain constructive on sovereign resilience and AZN stability, while 
seeing the ability to convert hydrocarbon wealth into a deeper pool of private 
investment and investable non-oil assets as the main medium-term challenge. 
Economic activity: Recovering now, but long-term growth limited 
GDP growth is currently into the second year of below-average growth, dragged down 
by an underperforming fuel sector at the beginning of the year, reflecting both 
structural and temporary factors. At the same time, higher hydrocarbon prices support 
financial inflows, which pass through to the wider economy, supporting confidence in 
the non-fuel sectors. Retail trade and non-fuel industry output have been accelerating 
throughout 2026 amid faster lending activity.  
While the consolidated budget remains in surplus thanks to fuel revenues, the stable 
non-oil deficit supports domestic demand. Following the extended fuel-led slowdown of 
economic activity in 2025-26, we expect growth to recover in the medium term. 
However, the potential growth rate appears capped at 2-3% unless economic 
diversification accelerates. 
Fig 20 Azerbaijan: GDP growth by key contributors  Fig 21 Azerbaijan: GDP growth by sectors

Source: National sources, CEIC, ING  Source: National sources, CEIC, ING

Household spending relies increasingly on retail credit, as positive real income growth is 
insufficient to cover consumption. On the corporate side, the fuel sector still weighs on 
industrial growth, but the recent rise in corporate lending suggests investment appetite 
elsewhere may recover.

Budget policy is benefiting from higher oil price environment 
Azerbaijan’s fiscal stance remains robust, though not growth restrictive. On the revenue 
side, fuel receipts are broadly stable, as lower export volumes are being offset by higher 
prices. Non-fuel revenues are also largely stable. On the expenditure side, higher social 
and defence spending, alongside persistently high investment outlays, continue to 
underpin spending pressures. The war in neighbouring Iran creates upside risks to 
defence and security spending.  
As a result, the consolidated budget remains in a comfortable surplus of around 2% of 
GDP thanks to fuel revenues, while non-fuel budget is in stable c.10% GDP deficit. 
Azerbaijan’s fuel fundamentals remain solid, with annualised revenues per US$1/bbl of 
oil price at US$150-200 million. This has helped keep the fiscal breakeven oil price stable 
at around US$59/bbl last year, even as spending has increased. Even assuming some 
increase in the fiscal breakeven in the coming years due to continued spending growth, 
ING’s house view on oil prices suggests that Azerbaijan will remain in fiscal surplus on a 
three-year horizon. 
For investors, the bigger issue is asset creation. Strong public finances limit sovereign 
issuance, leaving the investment story dependent on new non-oil projects and private-
sector borrowers.  
Fig 24 Azerbaijan: Consolidated budget balance  Fig 25 Azerbaijan: Budget sensitivities to oil prices

Source: National sources, CEIC, ING  Source: National sources, CEIC, ING

Inflation and monetary policy: CPI risks manageable, rates on hold 
Inflation risks have recently shifted somewhat to the upside, as food prices and higher 
global transport and commodity costs have renewed pressure on Azerbaijan’s producer 
price index. However, the exchange-rate peg remains a strong nominal anchor, limiting 
pass-through to consumer prices. With CPI still within the 4±2% target range, we expect 
the refinancing rate to remain on hold over the medium term.

However, further imported inflation linked to developments in the Middle East is the

Fig 26 Azerbaijan: Inflation and key rate  Fig 27 Azerbaijan: Inflation drivers

Balance of payments and AZN: External buffers reinforced by oil 
As on the fiscal side, higher oil prices have materially improved the external outlook, 
reversing much of the deterioration seen in 2025. As each US$10/bbl of oil price adds 
annual exports of around 4% of GDP, in 2026F the current account surplus may reach 
12% of GDP. In the meantime, sovereign assets equivalent to 115% of GDP keep growing 
and continue to provide substantial protection against potential external shocks. These 
buffers remain the main support for the AZN peg and broader macro stability.  
Over the longer term, however, declining hydrocarbon production and limited export 
diversification are likely to gradually erode the external surplus. Azerbaijan remains one 
of the region’s strongest external stories, although commodity dependence continues to 
shape its medium-term outlook. 
Fig 28 Azerbaijan: 12M exports by region  Fig 29 Azerbaijan: External sovereign asset position
